# Research Group subset-b-000595

Grouped research for ceph-client Linux networking Devicetree binding YAML schemas covering PCS blocks, PSE/PoE controllers, Qualcomm/QCA/Realtek/Renesas/Rockchip/Synopsys/TI Ethernet components, PHYs, MDIO, SFP, RFKill, and DWMAC-family wrappers. Each source file section is delimited for deterministic splitting into source-tree-aligned per-file research documents.

<!-- BEGIN_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/pcs/mediatek,sgmiisys.yaml -->
# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/pcs/mediatek,sgmiisys.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/pcs/mediatek,sgmiisys.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `MediaTek SGMIISYS Controller`, a Ethernet PCS binding. The schema description says: The MediaTek SGMIISYS controller provides a SGMII PCS and some clocks to the ethernet subsystem to which it is attached. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable networking code. Its purpose is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources and topology expected by the matching kernel subsystem before drivers probe hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses `oneOf` with 2 branches with 10 tokens: `mediatek,mt7622-sgmiisys`, `mediatek,mt7629-sgmiisys`, `mediatek,mt7981-sgmiisys_0`, `mediatek,mt7981-sgmiisys_1`, `mediatek,mt7986-sgmiisys_0`, `mediatek,mt7986-sgmiisys_1`, `syscon`, `mediatek,mt7988-sgmiisys0`, `mediatek,mt7988-sgmiisys1`, `simple-mfd`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `#clock-cells`, `mediatek,pnswap`, `pcs`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `#clock-cells`; required properties collected from nested branches include `#clock-cells`, `clock-names`, `clocks`, `compatible`, `pcs`, `reg`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`, `maxItems=3`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, MDIO child addresses, SPI/I2C addresses, or port selectors. The highest-risk ABI details are PCS register placement, protocol mode selection, phandle use by MAC nodes, and in-band status/AN expectations.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime the YAML does not execute: a compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then netdev, phylib/phylink, MDIO, DSA/switchdev, NFC, IEEE 802.15.4, regulator, clock, reset, GPIO, IRQ, DMA, IOMMU, and platform-bus helpers consume the validated resources. Child-node control flow is declarative too: MDIO children, switch ports, PHY packages, connector/LED nodes, and graph/fixed-link nodes are discovered by common Devicetree helpers according to the schema shape.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including PCS link negotiation state, SerDes mode state, phylink callbacks, and carrier status.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Matthias Brugger <matthias.bgg@gmail.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/phy/phy-common-props.yaml#`. Integration points include phylink, Lynx PCS users, Ethernet MAC drivers, SerDes lanes, MDIO/syscon access, and fixed-link or in-band negotiation paths. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/clk/mediatek/clk-mt7622-eth.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/clk/mediatek/clk-mt7629-eth.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/clk/mediatek/clk-mt7981-eth.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/clk/mediatek/clk-mt7986-eth.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/clk/mediatek/clk-mt7988-eth.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/mediatek/mt7629.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/mediatek/mt7622.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/mediatek/mt7981b.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/mediatek/mt7986a.dtsi`. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. Child-node schemas need special care because port numbering, PHY package topology, MDIO mux selectors, NFC transport GPIOs, and switch CPU/user-port layout must match driver tables. This schema rejects unknown top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. It has 5 top-level properties and 7 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/pcs/mediatek,sgmiisys.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/pcs/mediatek,sgmiisys.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, PHY links, MDIO children, switch ports, or GPIOs; use invalid child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/pcs/mediatek,sgmiisys.yaml -->

Clause 45 registers set. The PCS can be optionally synthesized with a vendor-specific interface connected to Synopsys PMA (also called DesignWare Consumer/Enterprise PHY) although in general it can be used to communicate with any compatible PHY. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/pse-pd/pse-controller.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/pse-pd/pse-controller.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Power Sourcing Equipment (PSE).`, a Ethernet MAC/controller binding. The schema description says: Binding for the Power Sourcing Equipment (PSE) as defined in the IEEE 802.3 specification. It is designed for hardware which is delivering power over twisted pair/ethernet cable. The ethernet-pse nodes should be used to describe PSE controller and referenced by the ethernet-phy node. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable networking code. Its purpose is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources and topology expected by the matching kernel subsystem before drivers probe hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses a composed compatible schema with 0 tokens: no explicit compatible constants. Top-level properties are `$nodename`, `#pse-cells`, `pse-pis`. Top-level required properties are none declared at the top level; required properties collected from nested branches include `#address-cells`, `#pse-cells`, `#size-cells`, `pse-pis`, `reg`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`, `minItems=1`, `maxItems=2`, `maxItems=4`. Important resource roles: properties are primarily direct dt-schema value constraints. The highest-risk ABI details are compatible fallback ordering, register/interrupt ordering, clock/reset names, DMA/coherency flags, PHY or fixed-link connection modelling, and vendor resource properties.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates `oneOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime the YAML does not execute: a compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then netdev, phylib/phylink, MDIO, DSA/switchdev, NFC, IEEE 802.15.4, regulator, clock, reset, GPIO, IRQ, DMA, IOMMU, and platform-bus helpers consume the validated resources. Child-node control flow is declarative too: MDIO children, switch ports, PHY packages, connector/LED nodes, and graph/fixed-link nodes are discovered by common Devicetree helpers according to the schema shape.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including netdev registration, DMA rings, NAPI scheduling, runtime PM, link mode negotiation through phylink/phylib, MAC address assignment, and PTP/TSN state where supported.

Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. Child-node schemas need special care because port numbering, PHY package topology, MDIO mux selectors, NFC transport GPIOs, and switch CPU/user-port layout must match driver tables. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/qca,qca7000.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/qca,qca7000.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Qualcomm QCA7000`, a Ethernet MAC/controller binding. The schema description says: The QCA7000 is a serial-to-powerline bridge with a host interface which could be configured either as SPI or UART slave. This configuration is done by the QCA7000 firmware. (a) Ethernet over SPI In order to use the QCA7000 as SPI device it must be defined as a child of a SPI master in the device tree. (b) Ethernet over UART In order to use the QCA7000 as UART slave it must be defined as a child of a UART master in the device tree. It is possible to preconfigure the UART settings of the QCA7000 firmware, but it's... It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable networking code. Its purpose is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources and topology expected by the matching kernel subsystem before drivers probe hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses a single `const` with 1 token: `qca,qca7000`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `qca,legacy-mode`. Top-level required properties are none declared at the top level; required properties collected from nested branches include `reg`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`, `minimum=1000000`, `maximum=16000000`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, MDIO child addresses, SPI/I2C addresses, or port selectors; `interrupts` routes link/status, RX/TX, error, or management IRQs. The highest-risk ABI details are compatible fallback ordering, register/interrupt ordering, clock/reset names, DMA/coherency flags, PHY or fixed-link connection modelling, and vendor resource properties.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime the YAML does not execute: a compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then netdev, phylib/phylink, MDIO, DSA/switchdev, NFC, IEEE 802.15.4, regulator, clock, reset, GPIO, IRQ, DMA, IOMMU, and platform-bus helpers consume the validated resources. Child-node control flow is declarative too: MDIO children, switch ports, PHY packages, connector/LED nodes, and graph/fixed-link nodes are discovered by common Devicetree helpers according to the schema shape.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including netdev registration, DMA rings, NAPI scheduling, runtime PM, link mode negotiation through phylink/phylib, MAC address assignment, and PTP/TSN state where supported.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Frank Li <Frank.Li@nxp.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/serial/serial-peripheral-props.yaml#`, `/schemas/spi/spi-peripheral-props.yaml#`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/flag`, `ethernet-controller.yaml#`. Integration points include Linux netdev Ethernet MAC drivers, phylink/phylib, MDIO buses, DMA/NAPI paths, clocks, resets, power domains, interrupts, IOMMUs, and board DTS MAC nodes. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/net/ethernet/qualcomm/qca_spi.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/net/ethernet/qualcomm/qca_uart.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/nxp/imx/imx6ull-tarragon-master.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/nxp/imx/imx6ull-tarragon-slave.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/nxp/imx/imx6ull-tarragon-slavext.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/nxp/mxs/imx28-duckbill-2-spi.dts`. The file includes 2 embedded examples for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. Child-node schemas need special care because port numbering, PHY package topology, MDIO mux selectors, NFC transport GPIOs, and switch CPU/user-port layout must match driver tables. This schema closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. It has 4 top-level properties and 8 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/qca,qca7000.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/qca,qca7000.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, PHY links, MDIO children, switch ports, or GPIOs; use invalid child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

The schema description says: This binding describes the Qualcomm IPA. The IPA is capable of offloading certain network processing tasks (e.g. filtering, routing, and NAT) from the main processor. The IPA sits between multiple independent "execution environments," including the Application Processor (AP) and the modem. The IPA presents a Generic Software Interface (GSI) to each execution environment. The GSI is an integral part of the IPA, but it is logically isolated and has a distinct interrupt and a separately-defined address space. See a... ## Control Flow

Child-node control flow is declarative too: MDIO children, switch ports, PHY packages, connector/LED nodes, and graph/fixed-link nodes are discovered by common Devicetree helpers according to the schema shape.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including netdev registration, DMA rings, NAPI scheduling, runtime PM, link mode negotiation through phylink/phylib, MAC address assignment, and PTP/TSN state where supported.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Alex Elder <elder@kernel.org>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle-array`. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. Child-node schemas need special care because port numbering, PHY package topology, MDIO mux selectors, NFC transport GPIOs, and switch CPU/user-port layout must match driver tables. This schema rejects unknown top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. It has 18 top-level properties and 18 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/qcom,ipa.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/qcom,ipa.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, PHY links, MDIO children, switch ports, or GPIOs; use invalid child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/qcom,ipq9574-ppe.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Qualcomm IPQ packet process engine (PPE)`, a Ethernet MAC/controller binding. The schema description says: The Ethernet functionality in the PPE (Packet Process Engine) is comprised of three components, the switch core, port wrapper and Ethernet DMA. The Switch core in the IPQ9574 PPE has maximum of 6 front panel ports and two FIFO interfaces. One of the two FIFO interfaces is used for Ethernet port to host CPU communication using Ethernet DMA. The other is used communicating to the EIP engine which is used for IPsec offload. On the IPQ9574, the PPE includes 6 GMAC/XGMACs that can be connected with external Ethernet... `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/qcom,qca807x.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Qualcomm QCA807x Ethernet PHY`, a Ethernet MAC/controller binding. The schema description says: Qualcomm QCA8072/5 Ethernet PHY is PHY package of 2 or 5 IEEE 802.3 clause 22 compliant 10BASE-Te, 100BASE-TX and 1000BASE-T PHY-s. They feature 2 SerDes, one for PSGMII or QSGMII connection with MAC, while second one is SGMII for connection to MAC or fiber. Both models have a combo port that supports 1000BASE-X and 100BASE-FX fiber. Each PHY inside of QCA807x series has 4 digitally controlled output only pins that natively drive LED-s for up to 2 attached LEDs. Some vendor also use these 4 output for GPIO usage... Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/realtek,rtl82xx.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/realtek,rtl82xx.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Realtek RTL82xx PHY`, a Ethernet MAC/controller binding. The schema description says: Bindings for Realtek RTL82xx PHYs It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable networking code. Its purpose is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources and topology expected by the matching kernel subsystem before drivers probe hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses an `enum` with 20 tokens: `ethernet-phy-id001c.c800`, `ethernet-phy-id001c.c816`, `ethernet-phy-id001c.c838`, `ethernet-phy-id001c.c840`, `ethernet-phy-id001c.c848`, `ethernet-phy-id001c.c849`, `ethernet-phy-id001c.c84a`, `ethernet-phy-id001c.c862`, `ethernet-phy-id001c.c878`, `ethernet-phy-id001c.c880`, `ethernet-phy-id001c.c910`, `ethernet-phy-id001c.c912`, `ethernet-phy-id001c.c913`, `ethernet-phy-id001c.c914`, `ethernet-phy-id001c.c915`, `ethernet-phy-id001c.c916`, `ethernet-phy-id001c.c942`, `ethernet-phy-id001c.c961`, `ethernet-phy-id001c.cad0`, `ethernet-phy-id001c.cb00`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `leds`, `realtek,aldps-enable`, `realtek,clkout-disable`, `realtek,clkout-ssc-enable`, `realtek,rxc-ssc-enable`, `realtek,sysclk-ssc-enable`, `wakeup-source`. Top-level required properties are none declared at the top level; required properties collected from nested branches include none found. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include no explicit numeric or item limits collected. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key. The highest-risk ABI details are compatible fallback ordering, register/interrupt ordering, clock/reset names, DMA/coherency flags, PHY or fixed-link connection modelling, and vendor resource properties.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime the YAML does not execute: a compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then netdev, phylib/phylink, MDIO, DSA/switchdev, NFC, IEEE 802.15.4, regulator, clock, reset, GPIO, IRQ, DMA, IOMMU, and platform-bus helpers consume the validated resources. Child-node control flow is declarative too: MDIO children, switch ports, PHY packages, connector/LED nodes, and graph/fixed-link nodes are discovered by common Devicetree helpers according to the schema shape.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including netdev registration, DMA rings, NAPI scheduling, runtime PM, link mode negotiation through phylink/phylib, MAC address assignment, and PTP/TSN state where supported.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Andrew Lunn <andrew@lunn.ch>, Florian Fainelli <f.fainelli@gmail.com>, Heiner Kallweit <hkallweit1@gmail.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `ethernet-phy.yaml#`. Integration points include Linux netdev Ethernet MAC drivers, phylink/phylib, MDIO buses, DMA/NAPI paths, clocks, resets, power domains, interrupts, IOMMUs, and board DTS MAC nodes. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: no direct in-tree driver match found by compatible scan. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. Child-node schemas need special care because port numbering, PHY package topology, MDIO mux selectors, NFC transport GPIOs, and switch CPU/user-port layout must match driver tables. This schema closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. It has 8 top-level properties and 8 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/realtek,rtl82xx.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/realtek,rtl82xx.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, PHY links, MDIO children, switch ports, or GPIOs; use invalid child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `clocks`, `power-domains`, `resets`, `phy-mode`, `phy-handle`; required properties collected from nested branches include `clocks`, `compatible`, `interrupt-names`, `interrupts`, `phy-handle`, `phy-mode`, `power-domains`, `reg`, `resets`, `rx-internal-delay-ps`, `tx-internal-delay-ps`. Patterned child schemas are `@[0-9a-f]$`. Numeric and collection constraints include `minItems=1`, `maxItems=29`, `maxItems=3`, `maxItems=1`, `minItems=2`, `minItems=29`, `minItems=25`, `maxItems=25`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, MDIO child addresses, SPI/I2C addresses, or port selectors; `interrupts` routes link/status, RX/TX, error, or management IRQs; `interrupt-names` routes link/status, RX/TX, error, or management IRQs; `clocks` binds MAC/MDIO/PHY/PTP/SerDes clock inputs; `clock-names` binds MAC/MDIO/PHY/PTP/SerDes clock inputs; `iommus` integrates the device with SoC PM, DMA, or IOMMU topology; `power-domains` integrates the device with SoC PM, DMA, or IOMMU topology; `resets` defines reset-controller or GPIO reset sequencing; `phy-mode` connects the MAC/switch port to PHY, PCS, SFP, or fixed-link infrastructure; `phy-handle` connects the MAC/switch port to PHY, PCS, SFP, or fixed-link infrastructure; `#address-cells` fixes child-node or phandle specifier shape; `#size-cells` fixes child-node or phandle specifier shape; `mdio` models MDIO management bus topology. ## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime the YAML does not execute: a compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then netdev, phylib/phylink, MDIO, DSA/switchdev, NFC, IEEE 802.15.4, regulator, clock, reset, GPIO, IRQ, DMA, IOMMU, and platform-bus helpers consume the validated resources. Child-node control flow is declarative too: MDIO children, switch ports, PHY packages, connector/LED nodes, and graph/fixed-link nodes are discovered by common Devicetree helpers according to the schema shape.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including netdev registration, DMA rings, NAPI scheduling, runtime PM, link mode negotiation through phylink/phylib, MAC address assignment, and PTP/TSN state where supported.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Sergei Shtylyov <sergei.shtylyov@gmail.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/net/mdio.yaml#`, `ethernet-controller.yaml#`. Integration points include Linux netdev Ethernet MAC drivers, phylink/phylib, MDIO buses, DMA/NAPI paths, clocks, resets, power domains, interrupts, IOMMUs, and board DTS MAC nodes. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/net/ethernet/renesas/ravb_main.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/renesas/r8a7742.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/renesas/r8a7743.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/renesas/r8a7744.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/renesas/r8a7745.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/renesas/r8a77470.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/renesas/r8a7790.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/renesas/r8a7791.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/renesas/r8a7792.dtsi`, and 2 more. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. Child-node schemas need special care because port numbering, PHY package topology, MDIO mux selectors, NFC transport GPIOs, and switch CPU/user-port layout must match driver tables. This schema rejects unknown top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. It has 18 top-level properties and 18 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/renesas,etheravb.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/renesas,etheravb.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, PHY links, MDIO children, switch ports, or GPIOs; use invalid child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `interrupt-names`, `resets`, `pcs-handle`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `interrupts`, `interrupt-names`, `resets`; required properties collected from nested branches include `clock-names`, `clocks`, `compatible`, `interrupt-names`, `interrupts`, `reg`, `reset-names`, `resets`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`, `minItems=7`, `minItems=10`, `minItems=15`, `maxItems=15`, `maxItems=3`, `minItems=19`, `minItems=2`, `maxItems=7`, `minItems=11`, `maxItems=11`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, MDIO child addresses, SPI/I2C addresses, or port selectors; `clocks` binds MAC/MDIO/PHY/PTP/SerDes clock inputs; `clock-names` binds MAC/MDIO/PHY/PTP/SerDes clock inputs; `interrupt-names` routes link/status, RX/TX, error, or management IRQs; `resets` defines reset-controller or GPIO reset sequencing; `pcs-handle` connects the MAC/switch port to PHY, PCS, SFP, or fixed-link infrastructure. The highest-risk ABI details are compatible fallback ordering, register/interrupt ordering, clock/reset names, DMA/coherency flags, PHY or fixed-link connection modelling, and vendor resource properties.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime the YAML does not execute: a compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then netdev, phylib/phylink, MDIO, DSA/switchdev, NFC, IEEE 802.15.4, regulator, clock, reset, GPIO, IRQ, DMA, IOMMU, and platform-bus helpers consume the validated resources. Child-node control flow is declarative too: MDIO children, switch ports, PHY packages, connector/LED nodes, and graph/fixed-link nodes are discovered by common Devicetree helpers according to the schema shape.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including netdev registration, DMA rings, NAPI scheduling, runtime PM, link mode negotiation through phylink/phylib, MAC address assignment, and PTP/TSN state where supported.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Lad Prabhakar <prabhakar.mahadev-lad.rj@bp.renesas.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `snps,dwmac.yaml#`. Integration points include Linux netdev Ethernet MAC drivers, phylink/phylib, MDIO buses, DMA/NAPI paths, clocks, resets, power domains, interrupts, IOMMUs, and board DTS MAC nodes. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/net/ethernet/stmicro/stmmac/dwmac-renesas-gbeth.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/renesas/r9a09g047.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/renesas/r9a09g056.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/renesas/r9a09g057.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/renesas/r9a09g077.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/renesas/r9a09g087.dtsi`. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. Child-node schemas need special care because port numbering, PHY package topology, MDIO mux selectors, NFC transport GPIOs, and switch CPU/user-port layout must match driver tables. This schema closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. It has 7 top-level properties and 9 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/renesas,rzv2h-gbeth.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/renesas,rzv2h-gbeth.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, PHY links, MDIO children, switch ports, or GPIOs; use invalid child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. It has 9 top-level properties and 9 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/smsc,lan9115.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/smsc,lan9115.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Smart Mixed-Signal Connectivity (SMSC) LAN911x/912x Controller`, a Ethernet MAC/controller binding. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable networking code. Its purpose is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources and topology expected by the matching kernel subsystem before drivers probe hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses `oneOf` with 2 branches with 6 tokens: `smsc,lan9115`, `smsc,lan89218`, `smsc,lan9117`, `smsc,lan9118`, `smsc,lan9220`, `smsc,lan9221`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `reg-shift`, `reg-io-width`, `interrupts`, `clocks`, `phy-mode`, `smsc,irq-active-high`, `smsc,irq-push-pull`, `smsc,force-internal-phy`, `smsc,force-external-phy`, `smsc,save-mac-address`, `reset-gpios`, `vdd33a-supply`, `vddvario-supply`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`; required properties collected from nested branches include `compatible`, `interrupts`, `reg`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`, `minItems=1`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, MDIO child addresses, SPI/I2C addresses, or port selectors; `interrupts` routes link/status, RX/TX, error, or management IRQs; `clocks` binds MAC/MDIO/PHY/PTP/SerDes clock inputs; `phy-mode` connects the MAC/switch port to PHY, PCS, SFP, or fixed-link infrastructure; `reset-gpios` defines reset-controller or GPIO reset sequencing; `vdd33a-supply` links board power rails through the regulator framework; `vddvario-supply` links board power rails through the regulator framework. The highest-risk ABI details are compatible fallback ordering, register/interrupt ordering, clock/reset names, DMA/coherency flags, PHY or fixed-link connection modelling, and vendor resource properties.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime the YAML does not execute: a compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then netdev, phylib/phylink, MDIO, DSA/switchdev, NFC, IEEE 802.15.4, regulator, clock, reset, GPIO, IRQ, DMA, IOMMU, and platform-bus helpers consume the validated resources. Child-node control flow is declarative too: MDIO children, switch ports, PHY packages, connector/LED nodes, and graph/fixed-link nodes are discovered by common Devicetree helpers according to the schema shape.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including netdev registration, DMA rings, NAPI scheduling, runtime PM, link mode negotiation through phylink/phylib, MAC address assignment, and PTP/TSN state where supported.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Shawn Guo <shawnguo@kernel.org>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/memory-controllers/mc-peripheral-props.yaml#`, `ethernet-controller.yaml#`. Integration points include Linux netdev Ethernet MAC drivers, phylink/phylib, MDIO buses, DMA/NAPI paths, clocks, resets, power domains, interrupts, IOMMUs, and board DTS MAC nodes. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings and phandle topology. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. The highest-risk ABI details are compatible fallback ordering, register/interrupt ordering, clock/reset names, DMA/coherency flags, PHY or fixed-link connection modelling, and vendor resource properties.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime the YAML does not execute: a compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then netdev, phylib/phylink, MDIO, DSA/switchdev, NFC, IEEE 802.15.4, regulator, clock, reset, GPIO, IRQ, DMA, IOMMU, and platform-bus helpers consume the validated resources. Child-node control flow is declarative too: MDIO children, switch ports, PHY packages, connector/LED nodes, and graph/fixed-link nodes are discovered by common Devicetree helpers according to the schema shape.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including netdev registration, DMA rings, NAPI scheduling, runtime PM, link mode negotiation through phylink/phylib, MAC address assignment, and PTP/TSN state where supported.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Jassi Brar <jaswinder.singh@linaro.org>, Ilias Apalodimas <ilias.apalodimas@linaro.org>. Direct schema dependencies are `ethernet-controller.yaml#`, `mdio.yaml#`. Integration points include Linux netdev Ethernet MAC drivers, phylink/phylib, MDIO buses, DMA/NAPI paths, clocks, resets, power domains, interrupts, IOMMUs, and board DTS MAC nodes. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/net/ethernet/socionext/netsec.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. Child-node schemas need special care because port numbering, PHY package topology, MDIO mux selectors, NFC transport GPIOs, and switch CPU/user-port layout must match driver tables. This schema closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. It has 7 top-level properties and 7 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/socionext,synquacer-netsec.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/socionext,synquacer-netsec.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, PHY links, MDIO children, switch ports, or GPIOs; use invalid child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/spacemit,k3-dwmac.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/spacemit,k3-dwmac.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Spacemit K3 DWMAC glue layer`, a Ethernet MAC/controller binding. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable networking code. Its purpose is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources and topology expected by the matching kernel subsystem before drivers probe hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses an ordered fallback `items` sequence with 2 tokens: `spacemit,k3-dwmac`, `snps,dwmac-5.40a`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `interrupts`, `interrupt-names`, `resets`, `reset-names`, `spacemit,apmu`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `interrupts`, `interrupt-names`, `resets`, `reset-names`, `spacemit,apmu`; required properties collected from nested branches include `clock-names`, `clocks`, `compatible`, `interrupt-names`, `interrupts`, `reg`, `reset-names`, `resets`, `spacemit,apmu`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`, `minItems=1`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, MDIO child addresses, SPI/I2C addresses, or port selectors; `clocks` binds MAC/MDIO/PHY/PTP/SerDes clock inputs; `clock-names` binds MAC/MDIO/PHY/PTP/SerDes clock inputs; `interrupts` routes link/status, RX/TX, error, or management IRQs; `interrupt-names` routes link/status, RX/TX, error, or management IRQs; `resets` defines reset-controller or GPIO reset sequencing; `reset-names` defines reset-controller or GPIO reset sequencing. The highest-risk ABI details are compatible fallback ordering, register/interrupt ordering, clock/reset names, DMA/coherency flags, PHY or fixed-link connection modelling, and vendor resource properties.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime the YAML does not execute: a compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then netdev, phylib/phylink, MDIO, DSA/switchdev, NFC, IEEE 802.15.4, regulator, clock, reset, GPIO, IRQ, DMA, IOMMU, and platform-bus helpers consume the validated resources. Child-node control flow is declarative too: MDIO children, switch ports, PHY packages, connector/LED nodes, and graph/fixed-link nodes are discovered by common Devicetree helpers according to the schema shape.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including netdev registration, DMA rings, NAPI scheduling, runtime PM, link mode negotiation through phylink/phylib, MAC address assignment, and PTP/TSN state where supported.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Inochi Amaoto <inochiama@gmail.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle-array`, `snps,dwmac.yaml#`. Integration points include Linux netdev Ethernet MAC drivers, phylink/phylib, MDIO buses, DMA/NAPI paths, clocks, resets, power domains, interrupts, IOMMUs, and board DTS MAC nodes. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/net/ethernet/stmicro/stmmac/dwmac-spacemit.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/riscv/boot/dts/spacemit/k3.dtsi`. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. Child-node schemas need special care because port numbering, PHY package topology, MDIO mux selectors, NFC transport GPIOs, and switch CPU/user-port layout must match driver tables. This schema closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. It has 9 top-level properties and 9 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/spacemit,k3-dwmac.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/spacemit,k3-dwmac.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, PHY links, MDIO children, switch ports, or GPIOs; use invalid child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/spacemit,k3-dwmac.yaml -->

Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, MDIO child addresses, SPI/I2C addresses, or port selectors; `interrupts` routes link/status, RX/TX, error, or management IRQs; `interrupt-names` routes link/status, RX/TX, error, or management IRQs; `clocks` binds MAC/MDIO/PHY/PTP/SerDes clock inputs; `clock-names` binds MAC/MDIO/PHY/PTP/SerDes clock inputs; `iommus` integrates the device with SoC PM, DMA, or IOMMU topology; `phy-mode` connects the MAC/switch port to PHY, PCS, SFP, or fixed-link infrastructure. The highest-risk ABI details are compatible fallback ordering, register/interrupt ordering, clock/reset names, DMA/coherency flags, PHY or fixed-link connection modelling, and vendor resource properties.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime the YAML does not execute: a compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then netdev, phylib/phylink, MDIO, DSA/switchdev, NFC, IEEE 802.15.4, regulator, clock, reset, GPIO, IRQ, DMA, IOMMU, and platform-bus helpers consume the validated resources. Child-node control flow is declarative too: MDIO children, switch ports, PHY packages, connector/LED nodes, and graph/fixed-link nodes are discovered by common Devicetree helpers according to the schema shape.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including netdev registration, DMA rings, NAPI scheduling, runtime PM, link mode negotiation through phylink/phylib, MAC address assignment, and PTP/TSN state where supported.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Swathi K S <swathi.ks@samsung.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `snps,dwmac.yaml#`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/tesla/fsd.dtsi`. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. Child-node schemas need special care because port numbering, PHY package topology, MDIO mux selectors, NFC transport GPIOs, and switch CPU/user-port layout must match driver tables. This schema closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. It has 8 top-level properties and 8 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/tesla,fsd-ethqos.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/tesla,fsd-ethqos.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, PHY links, MDIO children, switch ports, or GPIOs; use invalid child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

The schema description says: The 3-port switch gigabit ethernet subsystem provides ethernet packet communication and can be configured as an ethernet switch. It provides the gigabit media independent interface (GMII),reduced gigabit media independent interface (RGMII), reduced media independent interface (RMII), the management data input output (MDIO) for physical layer device (PHY) management. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable networking code. Its purpose is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources and topology expected by the matching kernel subsystem before drivers probe hardware.

Patterned child schemas are `^mdio@`. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`, `minimum=1`, `maximum=1024`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, MDIO child addresses, SPI/I2C addresses, or port selectors; `#address-cells` fixes child-node or phandle specifier shape; `#size-cells` fixes child-node or phandle specifier shape; `clocks` binds MAC/MDIO/PHY/PTP/SerDes clock inputs; `clock-names` binds MAC/MDIO/PHY/PTP/SerDes clock inputs; `interrupts` routes link/status, RX/TX, error, or management IRQs; `interrupt-names` routes link/status, RX/TX, error, or management IRQs. It has 6 top-level properties and 6 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/ti,davinci-mdio.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/ti,davinci-mdio.yaml` against boards using these nodes. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/ti,dp83822.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/ti,dp83822.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `TI DP83822 ethernet PHY`, a Ethernet MAC/controller binding. The schema description says: The DP83822 is a low-power, single-port, 10/100 Mbps Ethernet PHY. It provides all of the physical layer functions needed to transmit and receive data over standard, twisted-pair cables or to connect to an external, fiber-optic transceiver. Additionally, the DP83822 provides flexibility to connect to a MAC through a standard MII, RMII, or RGMII interface Specifications about the Ethernet PHY can be found at: http://www.ti.com/lit/ds/symlink/dp83822i.pdf It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable networking code. Its purpose is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources and topology expected by the matching kernel subsystem before drivers probe hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses a composed compatible schema with 0 tokens: no explicit compatible constants. Top-level properties are `reg`, `ti,link-loss-low`, `ti,fiber-mode`, `rx-internal-delay-ps`, `tx-internal-delay-ps`, `ti,cfg-dac-minus-one-bp`, `ti,cfg-dac-plus-one-bp`, `ti,rmii-mode`, `ti,gpio2-clk-out`, `mac-termination-ohms`. Top-level required properties are `reg`; required properties collected from nested branches include `reg`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`. Important resource roles: `reg` describes MMIO ranges, MDIO child addresses, SPI/I2C addresses, or port selectors. The highest-risk ABI details are compatible fallback ordering, register/interrupt ordering, clock/reset names, DMA/coherency flags, PHY or fixed-link connection modelling, and vendor resource properties.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime the YAML does not execute: a compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then netdev, phylib/phylink, MDIO, DSA/switchdev, NFC, IEEE 802.15.4, regulator, clock, reset, GPIO, IRQ, DMA, IOMMU, and platform-bus helpers consume the validated resources. Child-node control flow is declarative too: MDIO children, switch ports, PHY packages, connector/LED nodes, and graph/fixed-link nodes are discovered by common Devicetree helpers according to the schema shape.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including netdev registration, DMA rings, NAPI scheduling, runtime PM, link mode negotiation through phylink/phylib, MAC address assignment, and PTP/TSN state where supported.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Andrew Davis <afd@ti.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/string`, `ethernet-phy.yaml#`. Integration points include Linux netdev Ethernet MAC drivers, phylink/phylib, MDIO buses, DMA/NAPI paths, clocks, resets, power domains, interrupts, IOMMUs, and board DTS MAC nodes. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: no direct in-tree driver match found by compatible scan. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. Child-node schemas need special care because port numbering, PHY package topology, MDIO mux selectors, NFC transport GPIOs, and switch CPU/user-port layout must match driver tables. This schema closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. It has 10 top-level properties and 10 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/ti,dp83822.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/ti,dp83822.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, PHY links, MDIO children, switch ports, or GPIOs; use invalid child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/ti,icssm-prueth.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/ti,icssm-prueth.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Texas Instruments ICSSM PRUSS Ethernet`, a Ethernet MAC/controller binding. The schema description says: Ethernet based on the Programmable Real-Time Unit and Industrial Communication Subsystem. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable networking code. Its purpose is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources and topology expected by the matching kernel subsystem before drivers probe hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses an `enum` with 3 tokens: `ti,am57-prueth`, `ti,am4376-prueth`, `ti,am3359-prueth`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `sram`, `ti,mii-rt`, `ti,iep`, `ti,ecap`, `interrupts`, `interrupt-names`, `ethernet-ports`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `sram`, `ti,mii-rt`, `ti,iep`, `ti,ecap`, `ethernet-ports`, `interrupts`, `interrupt-names`; required properties collected from nested branches include `compatible`, `ethernet-port@0`, `ethernet-port@1`, `ethernet-ports`, `interrupt-names`, `interrupts`, `reg`, `sram`, `ti,ecap`, `ti,iep`, `ti,mii-rt`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=3`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `interrupts` routes link/status, RX/TX, error, or management IRQs; `interrupt-names` routes link/status, RX/TX, error, or management IRQs. The highest-risk ABI details are compatible fallback ordering, register/interrupt ordering, clock/reset names, DMA/coherency flags, PHY or fixed-link connection modelling, and vendor resource properties.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime the YAML does not execute: a compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then netdev, phylib/phylink, MDIO, DSA/switchdev, NFC, IEEE 802.15.4, regulator, clock, reset, GPIO, IRQ, DMA, IOMMU, and platform-bus helpers consume the validated resources. Child-node control flow is declarative too: MDIO children, switch ports, PHY packages, connector/LED nodes, and graph/fixed-link nodes are discovered by common Devicetree helpers according to the schema shape.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including netdev registration, DMA rings, NAPI scheduling, runtime PM, link mode negotiation through phylink/phylib, MAC address assignment, and PTP/TSN state where supported.

Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings and phandle topology. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 3 embedded examples for dt-schema validation.

Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. Child-node schemas need special care because port numbering, PHY package topology, MDIO mux selectors, NFC transport GPIOs, and switch CPU/user-port layout must match driver tables. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: no direct in-tree driver match found by compatible scan. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. Child-node schemas need special care because port numbering, PHY package topology, MDIO mux selectors, NFC transport GPIOs, and switch CPU/user-port layout must match driver tables. This schema rejects unknown top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. It has 2 top-level properties and 2 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/ti,pruss-ecap.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/ti,pruss-ecap.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, PHY links, MDIO children, switch ports, or GPIOs; use invalid child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/toshiba,visconti-dwmac.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Toshiba Visconti DWMAC Ethernet controller`, a Ethernet MAC/controller binding. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable networking code. Its purpose is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources and topology expected by the matching kernel subsystem before drivers probe hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses `oneOf` with 1 branch with 2 tokens: `toshiba,visconti-dwmac`, `snps,dwmac-4.20a`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `interrupts`, `interrupt-names`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`, `clock-names`; required properties collected from nested branches include `clock-names`, `clocks`, `compatible`, `reg`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, MDIO child addresses, SPI/I2C addresses, or port selectors; `clocks` binds MAC/MDIO/PHY/PTP/SerDes clock inputs; `clock-names` binds MAC/MDIO/PHY/PTP/SerDes clock inputs; `interrupts` routes link/status, RX/TX, error, or management IRQs; `interrupt-names` routes link/status, RX/TX, error, or management IRQs. The highest-risk ABI details are compatible fallback ordering, register/interrupt ordering, clock/reset names, DMA/coherency flags, PHY or fixed-link connection modelling, and vendor resource properties.

Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime the YAML does not execute: a compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then netdev, phylib/phylink, MDIO, DSA/switchdev, NFC, IEEE 802.15.4, regulator, clock, reset, GPIO, IRQ, DMA, IOMMU, and platform-bus helpers consume the validated resources. The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including netdev registration, DMA rings, NAPI scheduling, runtime PM, link mode negotiation through phylink/phylib, MAC address assignment, and PTP/TSN state where supported.
